2026-10-17T06:38:57.1770942Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [cluster:aspire2ap] [nodes:2] Move ASPIRE to P2/D2 with round-robin prefill DP dispatch and concurrency 88

The P3/D1 ASPIRE run 38796892855 (3895.05 good output tok/s) is bound
by its single decode worker on three fronts at once, measured from its
archived logs: running plus in-transfer requests sit at 22 per DP rank
in 72% of samples, which is the SGLang req slot cap of
max_running 20 + extra_slots 2 + 1 (pool_configurator); KV usage is 0.85
of the 106496-token pool with only about 1.2 GB of VRAM left after
CUDA graphs; and per-request ITL regressed on concurrent running gives
0.12 ms per request, so filling the remaining slots pushes ITL p99 from
19.9 ms past the 20 ms SLA. Meanwhile the twelve prefill ranks queue in
under 10% of seconds. The H100 ceiling of P3/D1 is therefore about the
current score, and the Firmus kernel chain does not change it: between
Firmus 38664373784 and 38945855851 the decode MegaMoE kernel time is
unchanged (0.242 vs 0.247 ms per call) and the gain came from prefill,
which was the binding stage on Firmus.

Reassign node 1 entirely to decode: two TP4/DP4/EP4 prefill workers on
node 0, two identical decode workers on node 1. Each decode worker keeps
the measured pool (106496 tokens, 80 running, extra slots 2, graph list
to 20, memory fraction 0.96), so the H100 memory assumptions of
6d8d718 are unchanged; decode load drops to about 8 requests per rank
and ITL to about 14 ms. Prefill becomes the binding stage at an
estimated 22-24 req/s from eight ranks, based on the Firmus saturation
rate of 2.9 req/s per rank with the same kernels and the equal FP8
compute peak of H100. The P2/D2 MegaMoE path already completed on
ASPIRE in run 38791490269 (c40, 2320.67, TTFT 1.13 s, ITL 11.4 ms).

Because prefill now binds, apply the prefill-side changes with evidence:
the kernel chain image (SHA-256 e29dfde4..., +4% raw on prefill-bound
Firmus), and round_robin DP dispatch for prefill workers. total_requests
dispatch skews work toward DP0 on both clusters (ASPIRE 2.52 to 2.02
seq/s across DP0..DP3, Firmus 5.9 to 4.5); DP attention and MegaMoE step
all four ranks together, so an underfed rank is lost capacity, and the
official fixed-length workload makes strict rotation balanced. Decode
keeps total_requests; the firmus-dp-rank-query patch lets decode learn
the prefill rank, so prefill dispatch is free to change. The dispatch
method is read per role from aspire-serving.json by submit.sbatch and
verified from server-info after the run.

Set concurrency 88 from the closed-loop relation TTFT = (c - decoding)
/ throughput with about 2.7 s of decode time: at 20-24 req/s TTFT stays
between 1.0 and 1.7 s, and decode is not starved unless supply exceeds
24.8 req/s. Keeping 112 would put TTFT p99 over 3 s if supply is 20.

Expected: raw 4400-4800 tok/s, score 4300-4650. The remaining risk is
TTFT if H100 prefill capacity falls below 20 req/s; the ITL risk is
removed. One run decides; prefill queue depth, decode running and the
official score are the checks. Tests, Ruff, shell syntax, YAML and
actionlint pass. Analysis and rejected alternatives are recorded in
research/runtime-performance/2026-10-17-aspire-h100-bottleneck.md.
